# MISP object templates: observables and pivotable identifiers (research notes for Fennec)

Research input for the Fennec observable/IOC data model (Rust, DDD + hexagonal + CQRS, PostgreSQL, OpenCTI/STIX 2.1 compatible, no generic types).

## 0. Provenance, method and legend

- Source clone: `.misp-objects` at commit `a063afec447d000911d667843c7493d914116ffa`; 453 templates under `objects/*/definition.json`.
- MISP core cross-reference: `.misp` at commit `fc99c9e3fc85beddc6ef18835359f3c8b7f44608`, file `app/Lib/Tools/AttributeValidationTool.php` (attribute normalisation and validation).
- STIX 2.1 SCO schema names cross-checked in `.stix2-json-schemas/schemas/observables/` (artifact, autonomous-system, directory, domain-name, email-addr, email-message, file, ipv4-addr, ipv6-addr, mac-addr, mutex, network-traffic, process, software, url, user-account, windows-registry-key, x509-certificate).
- Every attribute table below was generated by a script directly from the JSON (line numbers are 1-based lines in `objects/<name>/definition.json`; the files are `jq -S` normalised, so attributes are alphabetically sorted). Hand-written annotations were checked by the script against the JSON (every referenced template/attribute exists).
- Paths without prefix are relative to the `.misp-objects` clone root; MISP core paths are relative to the `.misp` clone root.
- Em dashes found in source descriptions are rendered as hyphens.

Legend for annotations:

| Tag | Meaning |
|---|---|
| [src] | Stated in the template itself (description, values) |
| [misp] | Behaviour of MISP core (`.misp/app/Lib/Tools/AttributeValidationTool.php`) |
| [ext] | Format from an external standard or my own knowledge; NOT in the source, verify before hard-coding |
| [obs] | My observation about a quirk, inconsistency or gap in the source |
| **Recommendation** | My design opinion for Fennec (sections 9 and 10 only, plus explicitly marked notes) |

Table columns: `L` = line of the attribute key; `M` = `multiple: true`; flags: `nocorr` = `disable_correlation: true`, `ids:false` = `to_ids: false`, `rec:false` = `recommended: false`; `VL` = `values_list` (closed list), `SD` = `sane_default` (suggested values, open).

## Contents

1. Key findings
2. How templates work (schema and conventions)
3. MISP core normalisation and validation per misp-attribute type
4. Template catalogue (observable-relevant templates, full attribute tables)
5. Borderline templates (identifier attributes only)
6. Excluded templates
7. Cross-cutting type quirks and inconsistencies
8. Deduplicated identifier kinds (final list)
9. Recommendations for Fennec
10. Open questions

## 1. Key findings

Source facts:

- 453 templates exist; 171 are catalogued here as observable-relevant (section 4, 2458 attributes, of which 1265 are typed `text`), 30 more are borderline (section 5). Excluded families: 51 `ftm-*`, 15 `regripper-*`, 6 `scrippsco2-*` (climate), 3 `covid19-*`, plus reports, incidents, impacts, rules and analysis metadata (section 6).
- The schema enumerates 193 `misp-attribute` types (`schema_objects.json` L39-L236). 62 of them are used by no template, including precise identifier types: `mutex`, `chrome-extension-id`, `mobile-application-id`, `windows-scheduled-task`, `cdhash`, `bin`, `jabber-id`, `named pipe`, `pgp-private-key`, `mac-eui-64`, `regkey|value`, `hostname|port`, `ip-dst|port`, `domain|ip`, `dkim-signature`, `azure-application-id` [obs]. The matching templates store those values as `text` (e.g. `mutex.name` L8, `browser-extension.id` L70, `apk.package-name` L47, `android-app.appid` L3, `scheduled-task.location` L32, `credit-card.iin` L28).
- Most platform and telecom identifiers are plain `text`: IMEI, IMSI, MSISDN, ICCID, TMSI, GUTI (`phone`), Telegram user id / bot token / chat id, Twitter numeric ids, Facebook/Instagram/Reddit/YouTube/Keybase/Google ids, JA4+ fingerprints, HHHash, MuonFP, Cobalt Strike watermark, Tor relay fingerprint, X.509 serial, PGP fingerprint and key id, purl, CPE components, container ids, ICAO24, MMSI, IMO, VIN [obs]. These are exactly the values Fennec must model as dedicated value objects (section 8).
- MISP core validation is shallow for many types (`btc`, `xmr`, `iban`, `bic` only `^[a-zA-Z0-9]+$`; `phone-number`, `cc-number`, `aba-rtn`, `bank-account-nr` only `is_numeric`; `url`, `favicon-mmh3`, `mobile-application-id`, `github-username` only reject newlines; `mutex`, `cpe`, `cookie`, `community-id` accept anything) [misp] (section 3). Its normalisation rules (lowercase hashes, strip colons from x509 fingerprints, AS prefix and asdot handling, MAC canonicalisation, phone `00` to `+`) are worth reproducing.
- Several templates are tuples or observation records rather than entities: `domain-ip`, `ip-port`, `network-connection`, `netflow`, `passive-dns`, `dns-resource-record`, `onyphe-service`, `modat-service`, `shodan-report`, `lookyloo-capture` [obs]. In Fennec they map to relationships/sightings between strongly typed observables, not to observable aggregates (recommendation).
- Type quirks that an importer must handle: `network-traffic.src_ip` typed `ip-dst`, `shodan-report.hostname` typed `domain`, `phishing-kit.phishing-domain` typed `url`, `cryptocurrency-transaction.address` typed `btc` for any chain, JA3S typed `ja3-fingerprint-md5`, `cert-pl-phishing.favicon-mmh3` base64 text vs Shodan-style integer elsewhere (section 7).
- Identity scoping is explicit in some templates: `chat.id` is "scoped to its service instance and parent container" (L24), `lookyloo-capture` is identified by instance + capture-id (required L121), GitLab users are instance scoped. Fennec identifiers for platform accounts need a platform/instance component (recommendation).
- Deduplicated identifier kinds found: 243 (section 8).

## 2. How templates work (schema and conventions)

Source facts (`schema_objects.json`, `README.md`):

| Element | Where | Semantics |
|---|---|---|
| `name`, `uuid`, `version`, `description`, `meta-category`, `attributes` | object properties L274-L326, required keys L328 | `version` is an integer; some templates use date-like versions (e.g. `bad-bot` 20260429, `shortened-link` 2018060501) [obs] |
| `meta-category` | L284-L303 | enum: `file`, `network`, `financial`, `marine`, `transport`, `misc`, `mobile`, `internal`, `vulnerability`, `climate`, `iot`, `health`, `followthemoney`, `detection`, `threat-hunting` |
| `required` | L307-L313; README L93 | array of attributes that must all be present |
| `requiredOneOf` | L314-L320; README L94 | at least one of these must be present |
| attribute `misp-attribute` | L39-L236 | one of 193 MISP types (case sensitive); required with `ui-priority` and `description` (L264-L268) |
| attribute `multiple` | L237; README L101 | several values of the same relation allowed |
| attribute `values_list` | L256; README L101-L102 | list of values that can be selected (closed list) |
| attribute `sane_default` | L243; README L102-L103 | recommended default values (open list) |
| attribute `disable_correlation` | L36; README L103 | suggests disabling correlation |
| attribute `to_ids` | L250; README L104 | can only be used to disable the IDS flag |
| attribute `recommended` | L240 (not documented in README) | used with `false` on insecure hashes and free text; `true` only on `apk.package-name` and 3 non-observable templates |
| attribute `categories`, `ui-priority` | L8, L253 | MISP UI categorisation and display priority |

Observed usage [obs]: `to_ids` appears 405 times, always `false` (0 `true`); `recommended: false` appears in 23 templates and `recommended: true` in 4. The README guidance (L550-L551) says `first-seen`/`last-seen` exist on every MISP object by default and recommends lax requirements via `requiredOneOf`.

## 3. MISP core normalisation and validation per misp-attribute type

Source: `.misp/app/Lib/Tools/AttributeValidationTool.php` (`modifyBeforeValidation` from L67, `validate` from L260). This is what MISP actually enforces for values carried by object attributes [misp].

| misp-attribute type(s) | Normalisation | Validation | Lines |
|---|---|---|---|
| md5, sha1, sha224, sha256, sha384, sha512, sha512/224, sha512/256, sha3-224/256/384/512, imphash, authentihash, pehash, ja3-fingerprint-md5, jarm-fingerprint, hassh-md5, hasshserver-md5, x509-fingerprint-md5/sha1/sha256, git-commit-id, dom-hash | lowercase; x509 fingerprints: colons removed then lowercase | exact hex length from `HASH_HEX_LENGTH` + `ctype_xdigit` (md5/imphash/ja3/hassh/dom-hash 32, sha1/pehash/git-commit-id/x509-sha1 40, sha224 56, sha256/authentihash/x509-sha256 64, jarm 62, sha384 96, sha512 128) | L5-L31, L74-L107, L183-L187, L263-L290, L301-L305, L717 |
| tlsh | lowercase | leading `t` stripped, then `strlen > 35` and hex | L291-L295, L694-L700 |
| telfhash | lowercase | length 70 or 72 and hex | L296-L300, L706-L709 |
| ssdeep | none | `^([0-9]+):([0-9a-zA-Z/+]*):([0-9a-zA-Z/+]*)$` | L306-L310, L736-L739 |
| impfuzzy | none | exactly two `:`, first part non-negative integer | L311-L318 |
| cdhash | lowercase | `^[0-9a-f]{40,}$` | L319-L323 |
| vhash | lowercase | `^.+$` | L620-L621 |
| ip-src, ip-dst | `normalizeIp` (IPv6 compression, CIDR) | `FILTER_VALIDATE_IP`; CIDR prefix <= 32 (v4) / <= 128 (v6) | L71-L73, L378-L400, L763 |
| port | none | integer 1..65535 | L401-L405, L685 |
| ip-src\|port, ip-dst\|port | tokenises `ip:port`, `[v6]:port`, `v6.port`, `v6 port N`, `v6pN`, `v6#N`, ambiguous `v6:N` | valid IP + valid port | L188-L216, L406-L415 |
| hostname, domain | lowercase; domain also trims `.` and converts IDN to punycode | `^[A-Z0-9.\-_]+\.[A-Z0-9\-]{2,}$` (case-insensitive) | L90, L108-L118, L425-L430, L678 |
| hostname\|port, domain\|ip | lowercase; `:` to `\|` (hostname\|port); IP normalised (domain\|ip) | domain regex + port / IP | L119-L134, L221-L223, L431-L448 |
| email, email-src, email-dst, target-email, whois-registrant-email, dns-soa-email, eppn, jabber-id | lowercase (first five only) | `^.[^\s]*\@.*\..*$` (case-insensitive) | L99-L103, L449-L461 |
| onion-address | lowercase | `^([a-z2-7]{16}\|[a-z2-7]{56})\.onion$` | L105, L416-L420 |
| mac-address, mac-eui-64 | lowercase, remove `.` `:` `-` space, colon every 2 chars | `^([a-f0-9]{2}:){5}[a-f0-9]{2}$` / `^([a-f0-9]{2}:){3}ff:fe(:[a-f0-9]{2}){3}$` | L217-L220, L421-L424 |
| AS | strip `AS` prefix; asdot `a.b` to `a*65536+b` | non-negative integer <= 4294967295 | L238-L251, L635-L639 |
| vulnerability | en dash to `-`; CVE and GCVE uppercased | `VULNERABILITY_REGEXES` (25 patterns: CVE, GCVE, GHSA, PYSEC, GSD, MAL, RHSA, cisco-sa, JVNDB, CNVD, CERT-FR, ...) | L33-L59, L158-L164, L462-L466 |
| weakness | en dash to `-`, uppercase | `^CWE-[0-9]+$` | L165-L167, L467-L471 |
| cc-number, bin | strip non-digits | `is_numeric` | L168-L170, L622-L630 |
| iban, bic | uppercase, strip non `[0-9A-Z]` | `^[a-zA-Z0-9]+$` (no checksum) | L171-L174, L614-L619 |
| btc, xmr, dash | none | `^[a-zA-Z0-9]+$` (no checksum) | L614-L619 |
| phone-number, whois-registrant-phone, prtn | `00` prefix to `+`, remove `(0)`, strip non `[+0-9]` | `is_numeric` | L175-L182, L622-L630 |
| bank-account-nr, aba-rtn | none | `is_numeric` | L622-L630 |
| http-method | uppercase | regex of 30 methods | L155, L324-L328 |
| hex | uppercase | `ctype_xdigit` | L156, L520-L521 |
| windows-service-name, windows-service-displayname | none | length <= 256 and no `/` or `\` | L472-L477 |
| ssh-fingerprint | none | `SHA256:` + base64 decoding to 32 bytes, or optional `MD5:` + 32 hex (colons removed) | L586-L590, L745-L758 |
| uuid | lowercase | `^[0-9a-fA-F]{8}(-[0-9a-fA-F]{4}){3}-[0-9a-fA-F]{12}$` | L106, L640-L641 |
| chrome-extension-id, edge-extension-id | none | `^[a-p]{32}$` (edge-extension-id is not in the misp-objects schema enum) | L642-L645 |
| link | none | must start with `http(s)://` and pass `FILTER_VALIDATE_URL` | L514-L519 |
| datetime | re-rendered ISO 8601 with microseconds, GMT | `strtotime` succeeds | L232-L237, L591-L595 |
| boolean | `true`/`false` to `1`/`0` | `== 1 \|\| == 0` | L224-L231, L633-L634 |
| counter, size-in-bytes / integer | none | non-negative integer / `FILTER_VALIDATE_INT` | L596-L601, L607-L612 |
| url, uri, user-agent, regkey, regkey\|value, filename, pdb, windows-scheduled-task, whois-registrant-name/org, whois-registrar, email-* header types, github-username/repository/organisation, twitter-id, dkim, dkim-signature, favicon-mmh3, mobile-application-id, azure-application-id, named pipe, identity/travel types | none | only rejects newline characters | L522-L585 |
| mutex, process-state, community-id, cookie, cpe, pgp-public-key, pgp-private-key, mime-type, identity-card-number, attachment, malware-sample, text, comment, other, pattern-*, yara, sigma, snort, suricata, zeek, stix2-pattern, kusto-query, names | none | always valid | L478-L513 |

Unused schema types (no template references them) [obs]: `azure-application-id`, `bin`, `bro`, `campaign-id`, `campaign-name`, `cdhash`, `chrome-extension-id`, `cortex`, `country-of-residence`, `dash`, `dkim-signature`, `domain\|ip`, `eppn`, `filename-pattern`, `filename\|authentihash`, `filename\|impfuzzy`, `filename\|imphash`, `filename\|md5`, `filename\|pehash`, `filename\|sha1`, `filename\|sha224`, `filename\|sha256`, `filename\|sha3-224`, `filename\|sha3-256`, `filename\|sha3-384`, `filename\|sha3-512`, `filename\|sha384`, `filename\|sha512`, `filename\|sha512/224`, `filename\|sha512/256`, `filename\|ssdeep`, `filename\|tlsh`, `filename\|vhash`, `frequent-flyer-number`, `gene`, `hostname\|port`, `ip-dst\|port`, `issue-date-of-the-visa`, `jabber-id`, `kusto-query`, `mac-eui-64`, `mobile-application-id`, `mutex`, `named pipe`, `passenger-name-record-locator-number`, `pattern-in-memory`, `pattern-in-traffic`, `payment-details`, `pgp-private-key`, `place-port-of-clearance`, `place-port-of-onward-foreign-destination`, `place-port-of-original-embarkation`, `primary-residence`, `prtn`, `regkey\|value`, `snort`, `special-service-request`, `travel-details`, `visa-number`, `whois-creation-date`, `windows-scheduled-task`, `zeek`.

## 4. Template catalogue

For each template: header, description, `required`/`requiredOneOf`, the full attribute table, then the list of attributes stored as `text` (or another loose type) although their content has a precise format: these are what Fennec must model strongly.

### 4.1 Files, executables and binary artefacts

#### `file`

- File: `objects/file/definition.json`; version 25; meta-category `file`; uuid `688c46fb-5edb-40a3-8273-1af7923e2215`; 37 attributes.
- Description: "File object describing a file with meta-information"
- `requiredOneOf` (L498): `filename`, `size-in-bytes`, `authentihash`, `ssdeep`, `md5`, `sha1`, `sha224`, `sha256`, `sha384`, `sha512`, `sha512/224`, `sha512/256`, `sha3-224`, `sha3-256`, `sha3-384`, `sha3-512`, `tlsh`, `telfhash`, `imphash`, `pattern-in-file`, `certificate`, `malware-sample`, `attachment`, `path`, `fullpath`

| L | object_relation | misp-attribute | M | flags | VL / SD | description |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3 | `access-time` | `datetime` |  |  |  | The last time the file was accessed |
| 8 | `attachment` | `attachment` |  |  |  | A non-malicious file. |
| 13 | `authentihash` | `authentihash` |  | rec:false |  | Authenticode executable signature hash |

Attributes with a precise format beyond their misp-attribute type:

- `fullpath` (L324, `text`, multiple): Absolute path including filename; OS specific syntax (Windows drive or UNC vs POSIX) [ext]
- `path` (L363, `text`, multiple): Complete or partial path [src]
- `magic` (L335, `text`): libmagic style file type description; classifier, not an identifier [ext]
- `file-encoding` (L45, `text`): IANA charset name (sane_default holds 258 IANA registry names) [obs]
- `state` (L456, `text`, multiple): Closed enum via values_list (Malicious, Harmless, Signed, Revoked, Expired, Trusted) [src]
- `text` (L476, `text`, multiple): Free text [src]
- `certificate` (L19, `x509-fingerprint-sha1`): Typed x509-fingerprint-sha1: the SHA-1 fingerprint of a signing certificate (non authenticode) [src]

- Remark [obs]: No cdhash, no code-signing team id, no ELF build-id or Mach-O UUID attributes exist in any file-family template (gap; MISP has a cdhash type that no template uses).

Attributes with a precise format beyond their misp-attribute type:

- `entrypoint-address` (L49, `text`): Entry point address as text (hex or decimal not specified); recommend u64 VO [obs]
- `entrypoint-section-at-position` (L55, `text`): Section name plus its index in the section table; separator not specified [src]
- `characteristics` (L8, `text`, multiple): IMAGE_FILE_* flag names (multiple); duplicated as characteristics-hex [src]
- `machine-type` (L101, `text`): IMAGE_FILE_MACHINE_* short names; duplicated as machine-type-hex [src]
- `lang-id` (L89, `text`): VERSIONINFO language id, e.g. 040904B0 (LANGID + codepage hex) [ext]

Attributes with a precise format beyond their misp-attribute type:

- `id` (L70, `text`): Chrome Web Store item id or Firefox AMO slug; raw id, not the store URL [src]. Chrome ids are 32 chars a-p [ext]; MISP core has chrome-extension-id with regex ^[a-p]{32}$ but the template uses text [misp]
- `permissions-added` (L96, `text`, multiple): Canonical extension API permission strings (webRequest, tabs, cookies) [src]
- `host-permissions-added` (L64, `text`, multiple): Host match patterns (MV3 host_permissions or MV2 match patterns), e.g. <all_urls>, *://*.example.com/* [src]
- `manifest-version` (L81, `text`): mv2 or mv3 [src]
- `version-clean` (L124, `text`): Extension version: 1 to 4 dot separated integers (Chrome manifest version format) [ext]
- `version-malicious` (L130, `text`): Extension version, same format as version-clean [ext]
- `ecosystem` (L42, `text`): chrome, firefox, edge (sane_default) [src]
- `publisher` (L102, `text`): Store publisher or developer account name [src]
- `attack-pattern` (L14, `text`): Short free text label [src]
- `analysis-source` (L3, `text`): manual, automated, vendor [src]

- Remark [obs]: The precise MISP type chrome-extension-id (regex ^[a-p]{32}$) is not used; Firefox ids (slug, GUID or email-like) would not pass it anyway, so Fennec needs one VO per ecosystem.

- Remark [obs]: Both watermark and license-id exist; attacker-infra uses license_id. Treat as one Cobalt Strike watermark VO.

Attributes with a precise format beyond their misp-attribute type:

- `id` (L21, `text`): Tracking code; format depends on tracker: Google Analytics UA-<4-10 digits>-<1-4 digits> (Universal), G-<10 alnum> (GA4), GTM-<alnum> (Tag Manager), AdSense ca-pub-<16 digits>; Piwik/Matomo integer site id; Kissmetrics/Woopra/Chartbeat vendor keys [ext]

- `description` (L3, `text`): Free text [src]

- Remark [obs]: requiredOneOf is only id; hostname is described as assumed safe and url as potentially malicious, i.e. the template mixes the identifier with where it was seen.

Attributes with a precise format beyond their misp-attribute type:

- `serial-number` (L211, `text`): Certificate serial: positive integer up to 20 octets (RFC 5280), usually hex [ext]
- `issuer` (L48, `text`): Issuer DN string (RFC 4514 style) [ext]
- `subject` (L226, `text`): Subject DN string [ext]
- `subject-common-name` (L231, `text`): Subject CN [src]
- `issuer-common-name` (L54, `text`): Issuer CN [src]
- `subject-key-id` (L267, `text`): SubjectKeyIdentifier, hex (typically 20 bytes) [ext]
- `issuer-key-id` (L90, `text`): AuthorityKeyIdentifier keyIdentifier, hex [ext]
- `issuer-serial-number` (L120, `text`): Issuer serial number [src]; probably the DN serialNumber attribute of the issuer, not the certificate serial (inference from naming) [ext]
- `subject-serial-number` (L297, `text`): Subject serial number [src]; probably the DN serialNumber attribute (inference from naming) [ext]
- `subject-country` (L237, `text`): C attribute, ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 [ext]
- `issuer-country` (L60, `text`): C attribute, ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 [ext]
- `pubkey-info-modulus` (L179, `text`): Hexadecimal, no 0x, no colons [src]
- `pubkey-info-exponent` (L174, `text`): Decimal [src]
- `pubkey-info-size` (L184, `text`): Decimal number of bits [src]
- `pubkey-info-algorithm` (L168, `text`): Public key algorithm name [src]
- `pem` (L163, `text`): PEM with Unix newlines [src]
- `raw-base64` (L190, `text`): DER base64 [src]
- `version` (L344, `text`): Certificate version (1, 2 or 3) [ext]
- `signature_algorithm` (L216, `text`): Algorithm name (sane_default 2 values) [src]
- `key_usage` (L150, `text`, multiple): KeyUsage names (multiple) [src]
- `extended_key_usage` (L15, `text`, multiple): EKU names or OIDs (multiple) [src]
- `rid` (L195, `text`, multiple): SAN registeredID: dotted OID (multiple) [ext]
- `text` (L321, `text`): Free text [src]

- `k` (L23, `text`): Key type, sane_default rsa (RFC 8463 adds ed25519) [ext]
- `public-key` (L38, `text`): Base64 public key (p= tag) [ext]
- `t` (L49, `text`): Flags y or s [src]
- `n` (L32, `text`): Notes [src]
- `version` (L59, `text`): DKIM1 [src]

Attributes with a precise format beyond their misp-attribute type:

- `fingerprint` (L24, `text`): Full fingerprint, upper case hex, no 0x, no spaces [src]; 40 hex for v4, 64 hex for v5/v6 [ext]
- `key-id` (L45, `text`): Hex key id without 0x; short ids collide, prefer the fingerprint [src]; short 8 hex or long 16 hex [ext]
- `algorithm` (L3, `text`): OpenPGP public-key algorithm identifier reported by the HKP index [src]; numeric ids per RFC 4880/9580 (1 RSA, 17 DSA, 18 ECDH, 19 ECDSA, 22 EdDSA) [ext]
- `flags` (L31, `text`): HKP flags r, d, e [src]
- `uid` (L73, `text`, multiple): User ID string "Name (comment) <email>" (multiple) [ext]

- Remark [obs]: All attributes to_ids false; template explicitly recommends linking pgp-meta via extracted-from for pivotable key ids, names and emails.

- `tmsi` (L69, `text`): TMSI: 32-bit value, 8 hex chars [ext]
- `guti` (L20, `text`): GUTI = GUMMEI + M-TMSI [src]
- `gummei` (L15, `text`): GUMMEI = MCC + MNC + MME Identifier [src]

- `MapSmsTP-PID` (L226, `text`): TP-PID octet [ext]

- `fingerprints-service-version` (L141, `text`, multiple): Service version [src]

| 3 | `datetime` | `datetime` |  | nocorr |  | Datetime |
| 12 | `signature` | `text` |  |  |  | Name of detection signature |
| 20 | `software` | `text` |  | nocorr |  | Name of antivirus software |
| 29 | `text` | `text` |  | nocorr |  | Free text value to attach to the file |

Attributes with a precise format beyond their misp-attribute type:

- `signature` (L12, `text`): AV detection name, vendor specific (CARO-like Type:Platform/Family.Variant) [ext]
- `software` (L20, `text`): AV product name [src]

#### `sb-signature`

- File: `objects/sb-signature/definition.json`; version 2; meta-category `misc`; uuid `984c5c39-be7f-4e1e-b034-d3213bac51cb`; 4 attributes.
- Description: "Sandbox detection signature"
- `required` (L43): `software`, `signature`

| L | object_relation | misp-attribute | M | flags | VL / SD | description |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3 | `datetime` | `datetime` |  | nocorr |  | Datetime |
| 12 | `signature` | `text` | yes |  |  | Name of detection signature - set the description of the detection signature as a comment |
| 21 | `software` | `text` |  | nocorr |  | Name of Sandbox software |
| 30 | `text` | `text` |  | nocorr |  | Additional signature description |

Attributes with a precise format beyond their misp-attribute type:

- `signature` (L12, `text`, multiple): Sandbox signature name (multiple) [src]
- `software` (L21, `text`): Sandbox product [src]

## 6. Excluded templates

252 templates are excluded because they describe reports, incidents, impacts, detection rules, analysis or case metadata, person/legal records, physical objects, media or climate data rather than pivotable technical observables. Families: `ftm-*` (51); `regripper-*` (15); `scrippsco2-*` (6); `covid19-*` (3).

## 8. Deduplicated identifier kinds (final list)

Each row is one distinct identifier kind. Tags in the format column say where each part of the format comes from ([src] template, [misp] MISP core, [ext] external standard or own knowledge, [obs] own observation). Sources list `template.attribute:Lline:misp-attribute`.

### 8.1 Content digests

| # | Identifier kind | Format / validation | Sources | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | MD5 digest | 32 hex; MISP lowercases and checks length (HASH_HEX_LENGTH md5=32) [misp] | `file.md5:L346:md5`, `pe-section.md5:L19:md5`, `elf-section.md5:L40:md5`, `macho-section.md5:L9:md5`, `lnk.md5:L173:md5`, `apk.md5:L30:md5`, `artifact.md5:L13:md5`, `hashlookup.MD5:L21:md5`, `iot-firmware.md5:L43:md5`, `stairwell.md5:L35:md5`, `cs-beacon-config.md5:L136:md5`, `cs-beacon-config.binary-md5:L26:md5`, `cs-beacon-config.config-md5:L56:md5`, `cs-beacon-config.jar-md5:L123:md5`, `attacker-infra.binary_md5:L61:md5`, `attacker-infra.config_md5:L100:md5`, `pcap-metadata.file-hash-md5:L93:md5`, `spearphishing-attachment.attachment-md5:L27:md5`, `onyphe-service.response-md5:L140:md5`, `uav.firmware-hash-md5:L44:md5` | The hashed subject differs (file, section, config blob, HTTP response, firmware); Fennec needs one Md5 VO but distinct owning observables. |

| 5 | SHA-384 digest | 96 hex [misp] | `file.sha384:L422:sha384`, `pe-section.sha384:L61:sha384`, `elf-section.sha384:L69:sha384`, `macho-section.sha384:L38:sha384`, `lnk.sha384:L214:sha384`, `apk.sha384:L94:sha384`, `iot-firmware.sha384:L66:sha384` |  |
| 6 | SHA-512 digest | 128 hex [misp] | `file.sha512:L428:sha512`, `pe-section.sha512:L67:sha512`, `elf-section.sha512:L75:sha512`, `macho-section.sha512:L44:sha512`, `lnk.sha512:L220:sha512`, `apk.sha512:L100:sha512`, `artifact.sha512:L53:sha512`, `iot-firmware.sha512:L72:sha512` |  |
| 7 | SHA-512/224 and SHA-512/256 digests | 56 hex and 64 hex [misp] | `file.sha512/224:L433:sha512/224`, `file.sha512/256:L439:sha512/256`, `pe-section.sha512/224:L72:sha512/224`, `pe-section.sha512/256:L78:sha512/256`, `elf-section.sha512/224:L80:sha512/224`, `elf-section.sha512/256:L86:sha512/256`, `macho-section.sha512/224:L49:sha512/224`, `macho-section.sha512/256:L55:sha512/256`, `lnk.sha512/224:L225:sha512/224`, `lnk.sha512/256:L231:sha512/256`, `apk.sha512/224:L105:sha512/224`, `apk.sha512/256:L111:sha512/256` |  |
| 8 | SHA3-224/256/384/512 digests | 56, 64, 96, 128 hex [misp] | `file.sha3-224:L398:sha3-224`, `file.sha3-256:L404:sha3-256`, `file.sha3-384:L410:sha3-384`, `file.sha3-512:L416:sha3-512`, `apk.sha3-224:L70:sha3-224`, `apk.sha3-256:L76:sha3-256`, `apk.sha3-384:L82:sha3-384`, `apk.sha3-512:L88:sha3-512`, `artifact.sha3-256:L41:sha3-256`, `artifact.sha3-512:L47:sha3-512` |  |
| 9 | RIPEMD-160 digest | 40 hex; no MISP type, stored as text [ext] | `pcap-metadata.file-hash-ripemd160:L99:text` | Only place where RIPEMD-160 appears. |
| 10 | SSDEEP fuzzy hash | MISP regex ^([0-9]+):([0-9a-zA-Z/+]*):([0-9a-zA-Z/+]*)$ (blocksize:hash:hash) [misp] | `file.ssdeep:L451:ssdeep`, `pe-section.ssdeep:L90:ssdeep`, `elf-section.ssdeep:L98:ssdeep`, `macho-section.ssdeep:L67:ssdeep`, `lnk.ssdeep:L243:ssdeep`, `apk.ssdeep:L123:ssdeep`, `artifact.ssdeep:L58:ssdeep`, `hashlookup.SSDEEP:L73:ssdeep` |  |
| 11 | TLSH | MISP: optional leading t stripped, then more than 35 hex chars [misp]; TLSH v4 digest is T1 + 70 hex [ext] | `file.tlsh:L484:tlsh`, `lnk.tlsh:L268:tlsh`, `apk.tlsh:L143:tlsh`, `artifact.tlsh:L63:tlsh`, `hashlookup.TLSH:L78:tlsh`, `stairwell.tlsh:L67:tlsh` |  |
| 12 | VirusTotal vhash | MISP only checks a non-empty single line (^.+$) [misp] | `file.vhash:L489:vhash`, `apk.vhash:L157:vhash` | Format proprietary; VO should be an opaque non-empty token. |

### 8.2 Executable structure

| # | Identifier kind | Format / validation | Sources | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 13 | Import hash (imphash) | 32 hex (MD5 of import table) [misp] | `file.imphash:L330:imphash`, `pe.imphash:L78:imphash`, `stairwell.imphash:L15:imphash` |  |
| 14 | impfuzzy | MISP: exactly two colons, first part positive integer (ssdeep style) [misp] | `pe.impfuzzy:L73:impfuzzy` |  |
| 15 | PEhash | 40 hex (MISP only supports SHA-1 pehash) [misp] | `pe.pehash:L161:pehash` |  |
| 16 | Authentihash | 64 hex (SHA-256 Authenticode hash) [misp] | `file.authentihash:L13:authentihash`, `pe.authentihash:L3:authentihash` |  |
| 17 | RichPE header hash | Typed md5 (32 hex) [src] | `pe.richpe:L184:md5` | Distinct semantics from a file MD5; needs its own VO/role. |
| 18 | telfhash (ELF symbol hash) | MISP: 70 or 72 hex chars [misp] | `file.telfhash:L471:telfhash` | Lives on file, not on elf. |
| 19 | PDB path | Free single-line string (pdb type) [misp] | `pe.pdb:L155:pdb` |  |
| 20 | PE VERSIONINFO strings | Free text resource strings; original/internal filename typed filename [src] | `pe.original-filename:L149:filename`, `pe.internal-filename:L83:filename`, `pe.company-name:L38:text`, `pe.product-name:L172:text`, `pe.file-description:L61:text`, `pe.file-version:L67:text`, `pe.product-version:L178:text`, `pe.legal-copyright:L95:text`, `pe.lang-id:L89:text` | Low-fidelity pivots. |
| 21 | Executable section name | PE: max 8 bytes; ELF/Mach-O: free [ext] | `pe-section.name:L25:text`, `elf-section.name:L46:text`, `macho-section.name:L15:text` | Weak pivot (packer section names such as UPX0). |
| 22 | Entry point address | Integer address stored as text [obs] | `pe.entrypoint-address:L49:text`, `elf.entrypoint-address:L186:text`, `macho.entrypoint-address:L3:text`, `ghidra-function.entrypoint-address:L31:text` | Not a standalone observable; u64 VO. |
| 23 | Ghidra BSim signature / Function ID FH and FX hashes / FLIRT hash | Hex (MISP hex type: ctype_xdigit, uppercased) [misp] | `ghidra-function.bsim-signature:L3:hex`, `ghidra-function.fid-fh-hash:L43:hex`, `ghidra-function.fid-fx-hash:L48:hex`, `ghidra-function.flirt-hash:L53:hex` | Code-similarity identifiers. |
| 24 | Mach-O cdhash | MISP regex ^[0-9a-f]{40,}$ exists but no template uses the cdhash type [misp] | (no template) | Gap in misp-objects. |

## 9. Recommendations for Fennec

Everything in this section is my own design opinion, derived from the facts above; nothing here is stated by the MISP sources.

### 9.1 Aggregates versus value objects versus relationships

| MISP construct | Fennec shape (recommendation) | Examples |
|---|---|---|
| Template whose identity is one strong identifier | Observable aggregate root keyed by a value object | `mutex` (MutexName), `tor-hiddenservice` (OnionAddress), `tor-node` (TorRelayFingerprint), `jarm` (JarmFingerprint), `tracking-id` (TrackingId with tracker), `android-app`/`apk` (AndroidPackageName), `browser-extension` (BrowserExtensionId per ecosystem), `container-image` (OciDigest), `pgp-key` (PgpFingerprint), `x509` (X509 SHA-256 fingerprint), `btc-wallet`/`coin-address` (CryptoAddress per chain), `credit-card` (Pan), `bank-account` (Iban or national account) |
| Template bundling several independent identifiers of one real thing | One aggregate plus typed VOs, each VO indexable for pivoting | `phone` (Imei, Imsi, Msisdn, Iccid, Tmsi, Guti): split into MobileEquipment (IMEI), Subscriber (IMSI), SimCard (ICCID) aggregates linked by relationships, because each pivots independently; `file` (all digests); `telegram-account` (TelegramUserId, TelegramUsername, PhoneNumber) |
| Tuple templates | Relationship or sighting with time window, not an aggregate | `domain-ip` (resolves-to), `ip-port` (network endpoint / service), `passive-dns` and `dns-resource-record` (DnsResolution), `c2-list` (ip:port endpoints) |
| Observation records from scanners and sandboxes | Observation/sighting events attached to existing observables; raw JSON kept as an artifact | `onyphe-service`, `modat-service`, `shodan-report`, `lookyloo-capture`, `cowrie`, `virustotal-submission` |
| Fingerprints shared by many hosts | Standalone observable aggregates (pivot = "who else shares this value") | JA3, JA3S, JA4 family, JARM, HASSH, HASSHServer, favicon MMH3, DOM hash, HHHash, MuonFP, Cobalt Strike watermark, RSA modulus |
| Per-object metadata | Plain fields on the aggregate, typed (no `text` catch-all) | PE header fields, ELF/Mach-O enums (closed Rust enums from the `sane_default` lists), counts, booleans |
| Secrets | Dedicated secret VOs, encrypted at rest, redacted in domain events and logs | passwords, API keys, Telegram bot tokens, card security codes, PEM private keys, malware config passwords |

### 9.2 Value object catalogue (sketch)

All VOs: private inner representation, `TryFrom<&str>` smart constructor applying normalisation then validation, canonical `Display`, `serde` as canonical string, PostgreSQL storage as canonical text or `bytea` for fixed-size digests.

- Digests: `Md5([u8;16])`, `Sha1([u8;20])`, `Sha224([u8;28])`, `Sha256([u8;32])`, `Sha384([u8;48])`, `Sha512([u8;64])`, `Sha512_224`, `Sha512_256`, `Sha3_224/256/384/512`, `Ripemd160([u8;20])`, `Ssdeep { block_size: u32, chunk: String, double_chunk: String }`, `Tlsh` (T1 + 70 hex canonical), `Imphash` (newtype over Md5 to keep the role), `Impfuzzy`, `PeHash` (Sha1 role), `Authentihash` (Sha256 role), `RichPeHash` (Md5 role), `Telfhash`, `VtVhash` (opaque non-empty single line), `DomHash` (Md5-sized), `OciDigest { algorithm, bytes }`.
- Network: `Ipv4Addr`/`Ipv6Addr` (std), `IpNetwork` (separate from addresses), `DomainName` and `Hostname` (lowercase, punycode, label rules; MISP regex as a floor), `Url` (parsed), `Port(NonZeroU16)`, `Asn(u32)` accepting `AS` prefix and asdot input, `MacAddr([u8;6])`, `CommunityId` (`1:` + base64 of 20 bytes), `OnionAddress` (v3 with checksum validation, v2 accepted read-only), `TorRelayFingerprint([u8;20])`, `TorNickname`.
- TLS/SSH/HTTP fingerprints: `Ja3([u8;16])`, `Ja3s([u8;16])` (distinct types even though both are MD5), `Ja4 { variant: Ja4Variant, value }` with one parser per variant (JA4, JA4S, JA4H, JA4L, JA4LS, JA4X, JA4SSH, JA4T, JA4TS, JA4TScan), `Jarm([u8;31])`, `Hassh([u8;16])`, `HasshServer([u8;16])`, `SshKeyFingerprint { Md5([u8;16]) | Sha256([u8;32]) }`, `SshPublicKey { key_type, blob }`, `FaviconMmh3(i32)` (accept base64 form on import from cert-pl), `HhHash { version: u8, digest: Sha256 }`, `MuonFp { window: u16, options: Vec<u8>, mss: u16, wscale: u8 }`, `X509Serial(Vec<u8>)` (max 20 octets), `X509Fingerprint*`, `DistinguishedName`.
- Email: `EmailAddress` (stricter than MISP: RFC 5321 addr-spec), `MessageId`, `MimeBoundary`, `DkimSelector` (missing in MISP, needed), `DkimRecord`.
- Host: `MutexName { namespace: Option<Global|Local|Session(u32)>, name }`, `RegistryKeyPath { root: RegistryRoot, path }`, `RegistryValueName`, `WindowsSid`, `ProcessId(u32)`, `ProcessGuid(Uuid)`, `ScheduledTaskPath`, `WindowsServiceName` (MISP rule: <= 256 chars, no slashes), `FilePath { os: Windows|Posix, raw }`, `FileName`, `CommandLine`, `DroidFileId(Uuid v1)` exposing derived MAC and timestamp, `VolumeSerial(u32)`, `NetbiosName`.
- Mobile/supply chain: `AndroidPackageName`, `AndroidPermission` (enum), `ChromeExtensionId` (`^[a-p]{32}$`), `FirefoxAddonId`, `Purl`, `Cpe23`, `SwidTagId`, `OsvId`, `ContainerId([u8;32])`, `ImageReference { registry, repository, tag, digest }`.
- Malware tooling: `CobaltStrikeWatermark(u32)`, `DgaSeed`, `RmmAgentId { product, id }`, `AvDetectionName { vendor, name }`.
- Telephony: `E164PhoneNumber`, `Msisdn`, `Imei([u8;15])` with Luhn and `Imeisv`, `Imsi { mcc, mnc, msin }`, `Iccid` with Luhn, `Tmsi(u32)`, `Guti`, `Gummei`, `Plmn { mcc, mnc }`, `Lac(u16)`, `CellId(u32)`, `GlobalTitle`, `PointCode { Itu(u16) | Ansi(u32) }`, `TadigCode`, `DiameterIdentity`, `DiameterRealm`, `DiameterSessionId`.
- Financial: `BtcAddress { P2pkh | P2sh | Bech32 | Bech32m, network }`, `MoneroAddress`, `EthereumAddress` (EIP-55), `CryptoTxHash { chain, bytes }`, `Pan` (Luhn, masked display), `Iin`, `Iban` (mod-97), `Bic`, `AbaRoutingNumber` (3-7-1 checksum), `NationalBankAccount { country, number }`, `BoletoNumber`, `FebrabanCode`.
- Accounts: `PlatformAccountId { platform, instance: Option<Host>, id }` is tempting but would be generic; instead one VO per platform: `TelegramUserId(u64)`, `TelegramUsername`, `TelegramBotToken { bot_id: u64, secret }`, `TelegramChatId(i64)`, `TwitterUserId(u64)`, `TwitterScreenName`, `TweetId(u64)`, `GithubLogin`, `GithubRepository { owner, name }`, `GithubNodeId`, `GitlabUser { instance, id, username }`, `FacebookId(u64)`, `InstagramUsername`, `RedditFullname`, `RedditUsername`, `SubredditName`, `YoutubeChannelId`, `YoutubePlaylistId`, `KeybaseUid`, `KeybaseUsername`, `GoogleGaiaId`, `ChatContainerRef { protocol, network, address, parent, id }`, `ChatMessageRef { container, id }`.
- Physical/radio: `Icao24(u32)`, `AircraftRegistration`, `Callsign`, `Squawk(u16)` octal, `Mmsi(u32)`, `ImoNumber` (check digit), `VesselCallSign`, `Vin`, `FccId`, `UasId`, `Ssid(Vec<u8>)`.

### 9.3 Normalisation to borrow from MISP core

Lowercase hex digests, hostnames, domains, emails and onion addresses; strip colons from X.509 fingerprints; trim dots and punycode domains; strip `AS` and convert asdot; canonical MAC; phone `00` to `+`, drop `(0)`, keep `[+0-9]`; IBAN/BIC uppercase and strip separators; card numbers strip non-digits; CVE/GCVE uppercase and en dash to hyphen (`.misp/app/Lib/Tools/AttributeValidationTool.php` L67-L251). Then apply stricter checksum validation than MISP where a standard defines one (Luhn, mod-97, Base58Check/Bech32, IMO check digit, EIP-55).

### 9.4 Validation policy for spoofed or malformed identifiers

CTI data legitimately contains invalid identifiers (spoofed AIS MMSI/IMO, fake IMEIs, typo-squatted handles). Suggested policy: reject on syntax (wrong alphabet or length), accept with a recorded `checksum_valid = false` property on checksum failure, because the invalid value is itself intelligence. This keeps types strong without silently discarding evidence.

### 9.5 MISP import adapter

- A static mapping table `(template name, object_relation) -> Fennec VO constructor` generated from section 4; unknown relations fail loudly instead of falling back to a generic text observable.
- Quirk rules from section 7 (e.g. `network-traffic.src_ip` is a source address, `shodan-report.hostname` is a hostname, `cert-pl-phishing.favicon-mmh3` is base64, `cryptocurrency-transaction.address` needs chain detection from `symbol`).
- `values_list` values become closed Rust enums; `sane_default` values become enums with an explicit `Other(String)` only where the template list is open (e.g. `coin-address.symbol`, `rmm.rmm-type`), or a reference-data table.
- Keep the original MISP object (uuid, template uuid and version) as provenance on the created aggregates to allow round-trip export.

### 9.6 STIX 2.1 / OpenCTI alignment

STIX 2.1 SCOs present in `.stix2-json-schemas/schemas/observables/` cover only a minority of the kinds in section 8 (file, artifact, directory, domain-name, ipv4-addr, ipv6-addr, mac-addr, url, email-addr, email-message, autonomous-system, mutex, network-traffic, process, software, user-account, windows-registry-key, x509-certificate). Telephony, platform accounts, fingerprints, crypto addresses, payment and bank identifiers, devices and radio identifiers need custom observable types or extension definitions; cross-check with the OpenCTI custom observable list before naming them.

## 10. Open questions

1. Should shared fingerprints (JA3/JA4/JARM/HASSH/favicon MMH3/CS watermark) be standalone observable aggregates, or typed attributes of a NetworkService/Certificate aggregate? Needs alignment with how OpenCTI models them.
2. How to type identifiers whose format is not public or stable (VirusTotal vhash and submitter id, Parler ids, RMM agent GUIDs, EDR endpoint ids, Cytomic machine UID): opaque but dedicated VOs with minimal rules (non-empty, single line, max length)? Does that satisfy the "no generic types" rule?
3. Reject or keep spoofed/checksum-invalid identifiers (section 9.4)?
4. Should secrets (passwords, tokens, private keys, CVV) be observables at all, or live in a separate, access-controlled bounded context?
5. One aggregate per social platform, or one Account aggregate with a closed platform enum and per-platform id VOs? `microblog.type`, `chat.protocol` and `instant-message.app-used` show MISP mixing platforms in one template.
6. JA4+ grammars (especially JA4L, JA4LS, JA4SSH, JA4TScan) and licensing (JA4 BSD vs other JA4+ FoxIO License) must be verified against the FoxIO specification before writing parsers.
7. Formats tagged [ext] and marked "verify" (VirusTotal submitter id, EU drone operator id, Keybase uid, Parler ids, Facebook/Instagram id ranges) need confirmation from primary specifications.
8. `ip-port` carries `ip`, `ip-src` and `ip-dst` simultaneously; which direction semantics should the importer assign when only `ip` is present?
9. MISP lacks DKIM selector, Mach-O cdhash/team id, ELF build-id, YouTube video id and Reddit post/comment ids; should Fennec add these from the start even though MISP import cannot populate them?
